## management-governance — module synthesis

# Management-Governance Module — SMPL (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Simply Good Foods presents a mixed stewardship picture, not a clean case for or against trust. The five-year capital-allocation record is dragged down by the $280.2M, debt-funded OWYN acquisition, 71% of which was written off within 24 months — a serial-acquirer pattern (with the earlier $982.1M Quest deal) that caps the capital-allocation score — though a genuine 2026 buyback surge ($213.2M at an $18.29 average, falling to $12.14) reversed four prior years of dilution and was corroborated by real insider buying. Incentives are split: a per-share relative-TSR long-term award sits alongside a 100%-size-based annual bonus and an unconditioned RSU sleeve. Insider ownership is thin (8.76%, mostly a costless legacy stake) but net insider buying is real and post-dates bad news. The biggest red flag is the OWYN write-off itself, occurring alongside three senior-leadership departures in twelve months. Management has been candid about both failures on verbatim transcripts. Verdict: standard/mixed stewardship, watchlist-band governance.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** Standard / mixed
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — all eight hard disqualifiers checked clean (unqualified Deloitte & Touche audit opinion, no going-concern note, 0% insider pledging vs the 50% threshold, ~0% related-party transactions vs the 25% threshold, zero auditor changes since 2019, no restatement, no active enforcement action, no >40%-single-customer concentration, no negative-operating-cash-flow pattern) [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md §1, §3]. No verdict-lock applies from this cross-reference.
- **Management quality /100:** 44 (mixed-to-weak band; see `01_management-and-track-record.md`)
- **Capital allocation /100:** 47 (mixed; RF-CAP-004 cap of 50 stated but not the binding constraint — the uncapped component sum already sits below it; see `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`)
- **Incentive alignment /100:** 68 (watchlist band; see `03_incentives-and-compensation.md`)
- **Shareholder friendliness /100:** 76 (average of `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`'s Ownership Alignment Score, 75/100, and `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`'s Board & Shareholder Rights Score, 76/100 — this module reports the two component scores separately below and rolls them into one "shareholder friendliness" figure per the MODULE_RULES composite formula, which defines a single score of this name)
- **Disclosure candor /100:** 75 (strong band, lower half; see `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`)
- **Governance risk /100** *(inverted — higher = worse)*: 62 — set above the RF-CAP-004 floor of 60 (§24 Filter 4) to reflect the accumulated evidence: the OWYN write-off itself, three senior-leadership departures in 12 months, a size-based-only annual bonus, recurring "one-off" non-GAAP add-backs that exclude a $391.9M recurring impairment pattern, and a charter/bylaw stack that denies shareholders the right to call a special meeting or act by written consent — offset by clean ownership structure, zero pledging, and near-zero related-party leakage
- **Data quality /100:** 88 *(from `00_governance-data-triage.md` — "Sufficient" verdict, no partial-data caps triggered; the deduction reflects the current CEO's post-return compensation package and ownership stake, which sit outside the data pool and are web-sourced/unverified, plus the unconfirmed 2026 AGM voting outcome)*
- **Overall usefulness /100:** 90
- **Insider ownership (one line):** 8.76% director/officer group as of Dec-1-2025 (proxy), ~73% of the largest individual stake (Chairman Kilts, 3.89%) is a costless 2022 legacy SPAC-sponsor distribution rather than cash-purchased; net open-market insider buying of ~$1.4M across four insiders in the trailing 12 months, concentrated after — not before — the Apr-2026 guidance-miss disclosure; the current CEO's post-return stake is not yet disclosed in the pool.
- **Biggest governance signal (one line):** The $280.2M, debt-funded OWYN acquisition (Jun-2024) had 71% of its purchase price impaired within 24 months, with the company's own goodwill test citing the market's own share-price decline as the trigger — the clearest, audited evidence of value-destructive capital allocation in the pool, occurring alongside three senior-leadership changes in twelve months.
- **Governance Score /100** — formula: `0.20×CapAlloc + 0.18×Incentive + 0.18×ShFriendliness + 0.16×Candor + 0.16×MgmtQuality + 0.12×(100 − GovRisk)`:
  `= 0.20×47 + 0.18×68 + 0.18×76 + 0.16×75 + 0.16×44 + 0.12×(100−62)`
  `= 9.40 + 12.24 + 13.68 + 12.00 + 7.04 + 4.56 = 58.92 ≈ 59`
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100:** `59 × 85 / 100 = 50.15 ≈ 50`
- **Governance Rating:** Watchlist *(55–69 band; 59 falls in this band)*
- **Confidence Score /100** (source quality): 85 — the large majority of findings rest on Tier-1/2 primary sources (the 2026 proxy, FY2025 10-K, two 10-Qs, two verbatim earnings-call transcripts, Form-4-sourced insider data), discounted for the current CEO's employment-agreement terms and post-return ownership, which are web-sourced/unverified (confidence 1–2) because the underlying 8-K exhibits are not in the data pool.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** 8 / 2

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation *(CLAUDE.md §8; fix F37)*

- **Strongest bear point:** The $280.2M OWYN acquisition (debt-funded, Board-approved) had 71% of its purchase price impaired within 24 months [FY26 Q3 10-Q, Note 4], the incremental capital it consumed earned roughly a 0% return before the write-down [`02` §4, finding 02-015], it is the second of two material debt-funded deals in six years (with 2019's ~$982.1M Quest purchase), triggering the §24 Filter 4 serial-acquirer cap, and it landed in the same 12-month window as the departure of the CEO who did the deal, his CFO's predecessor, and the Controller/CAO — with the departing CEO collecting a $3.465M–$5.2M severance package under a Change-in-Control multiplier the Board had raised only months earlier [`01` §4A, §5, finding 01-014].
- **Strongest bull point (steelman):** Management owned both failures in specific, unhedged language on verbatim transcripts ("our marketplace execution was poor and our brand performance fell well short of our plans") rather than deflecting to macro excuses [`06` §1]; the ownership and board structure are genuinely clean (single share class, no controlling bloc, zero pledging, ~0% related-party transactions, 97.3% say-on-pay support); and the FY2026 buyback program ($213.2M at an average $18.29, accelerating to $12.14 in the most recent quarter) is real, price-disciplined capital return, corroborated within $0.36 by two insiders buying their own stock in the same window [`02` §3, finding 02-012].
- **Single killer risk:** A third material, debt-funded acquisition before OWYN stabilizes — the exact pattern §24 Filter 4 is built to catch — combined with a still-undisclosed new-CEO pay package (a 2,000,000-share, purely time-vesting inducement option with no performance hurdle, web-sourced/unverified) that would formalize a shift away from the per-share TSR discipline used for the rest of the executive team [`03` §1, §4].
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** The FY2026 buyback reversed four straight years (FY21–FY24) of net share-count dilution [`02` §3, finding 02-008 through 02-010]; insider buying by the Chairman, CFO, an independent director, and the Chief Commercial Officer post-dates rather than pre-dates bad news [`04` §2, §4A]; and no RPT leakage, pledging, or dual-class structure exists to independently corroborate a misalignment thesis [`05` §3, §4].

## 3. Reconciliation

Two disagreements surface across the specialist reads, both resolved conservatively:

1. **Capital allocation vs disclosure candor.** `02_capital-allocation-scorecard` scores the OWYN deal near the bottom of its scale (1–2/20 component scores) while `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality` gives management credit (22/25) for candidly admitting the same deal's failure on two verbatim transcripts. Both reads are correct and not in tension: a team can be honest about a mistake and still have made a bad, value-destructive decision. This synthesis keeps them as separate scores rather than netting one against the other — candor about a miss does not repair the capital that was destroyed, and this module does not let the tone credit soften the capital-allocation cap.
2. **Ownership/board structure vs true board independence.** `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` and `05_board-and-shareholder-rights` both score the control structure as clean (no dual-class, no promoter, no RPT leakage — RF-OWN-004 explicitly not triggered), while `05` separately flags that 3 of the 10 nominally independent directors (including the Chair and Vice Chair) are current partners at the same outside firm, Centerview Capital Consumer, and jointly inherited the SPAC sponsor's board-nomination rights of unresolved current legal status. This is not a contradiction — a company can have no controlling owner and still have a board whose independence is more technical than substantive at three of its ten seats. The conservative reconciliation: shareholder friendliness is scored at 76 (strong-band, not "very strong") specifically because of this and the charter-level special-meeting/written-consent limitation (05-016), not because of any ownership-structure or RPT problem.

No other material disagreements exist between specialists — all six agree the OWYN acquisition is the single largest negative data point, all six agree ownership/board mechanics are structurally clean, and all six independently confirm no unresolved adverse integrity signal from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan`.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

This team has partially delivered on what it promised and partially destroyed value with the capital it was given. FY2023–FY2025 revenue and Adjusted EBITDA guidance was mostly kept or narrowly missed, but the two promises that mattered most — the departing CEO's final quarterly guide and the implicit growth thesis behind the $280.2M OWYN acquisition — were both broken badly, and the OWYN deal is the clearest, audited case of per-share value destruction in the record (71% impaired within 24 months, an incremental return near zero before the write-down). Incentives point management in two directions at once: the long-term PSU award is genuinely per-share (relative TSR), but the annual cash bonus that pays out every year rewards absolute size (net sales and EBITDA dollars, no ROIC/EPS/margin component), and half the long-term award (RSUs) has no performance bar at all. Minority shareholders are structurally protected — no controlling owner, no material related-party leakage, zero pledging, a single share class — but the board's independence is more technical than substantive at three of ten seats (the Chair, Vice Chair, and one more director share the same outside firm), and the charter denies shareholders the right to call a special meeting or act by written consent. Management has been candid about both major failures in specific, unhedged language on verbatim transcripts, which is a genuine, evidence-based positive; the counterweight is that the company's own "clean" profitability metric, Adjusted EBITDA, has structurally excluded a $391.9M impairment pattern that has now recurred in three of the last four quarters. The single most important reason for caution: this is a team whose one big, discretionary capital-allocation decision in the review period failed on its own terms, in the same 12-month window three senior leaders left, and whose primary annual incentive still does not require anything resembling per-share discipline to pay out.

## 5B. Peer Governance Benchmark

No peer set — relative governance not assessed. Every specialist's Universal Findings Table records "No peer set" against the peer-benchmark column for governance-specific metrics (board independence, insider/promoter holding, pledge, RPT intensity, AGM votes-against); `business-model/08_competitive-map` was not read by, or was not available to, this module's specialists as a governance peer-benchmark source. The one metric with an explicit relative comparator is pay-versus-performance: SMPL's FY2025 TSR ($112.76 per $100 invested) outperformed the S&P 500 Packaged Food & Meats index ($88.83 per $100 invested) [`03` §3, finding 03-008] — noted here because it is the only peer-relative data point available, not a substitute for the governance-metric peer table this section calls for.

No prior run — first governance snapshot. No prior dated run exists for SMPL under `analyses/SMPL_{prior-date}/management-governance/`, so no delta table can be produced. The single most important item to track at the next run: whether the 2026 AGM voting outcome (say-on-pay and director elections, post-CEO-transition) shows any material shift from the 97.3% FY2024 support level, and whether the current CEO's compensation package and post-return ownership stake — both currently web-sourced/unverified in this run — have been confirmed against the primary SEC Form 8-K exhibits.

## 5D. Analyst Follow-Up Questions

- **OWYN acquisition / serial-acquirer pattern (RF-CAP-004):** Is this a one-off failed bet or a recurring pattern? Material to future capital allocation and per-share value — yes, it is the largest capital-allocation decision in the review window. Is disclosure adequate? Yes (fully quantified in the 10-Q). Company-specific or sector-wide? Company-specific (execution/integration failure, per management's own words, not a category demand problem). Does management's explanation hold? Partially — management attributes the failure to "integration and execution issues rather than a lack of consumer demand," which is a specific, checkable claim, not yet independently verified by a subsequent recovery in OWYN's own numbers.
- **Non-GAAP exclusion of recurring impairments (RF-DISC-002):** Is the impairment pattern (3 of last 4 quarters) truly non-recurring, or should investors expect a fourth? Material to earnings quality and to the credibility of Adjusted EBITDA as the company's primary KPI. Affects minority holders directly, since compensation and guidance are both built around this metric. Follow-up: does a fourth impairment occur in FQ4 FY2026 or FY2027?
- **Turnaround inflection (RF-MGT-004):** Are the three consecutive quarterly beats evidence of a genuine inflection, or simply a beat against a materially lowered bar? Material to the entire investment thesis. Follow-up: does FQ4 FY2026 show YoY improvement (not just a beat) in net sales, gross margin, and Adjusted EBITDA?
- **Annual bonus design (RF-MGT-003):** Is the 100%-size-based bonus a legacy design about to be reformed under the new CEO, or a durable feature? Material to whether future capital allocation and operating decisions will be size-maximizing or per-share-maximizing. Follow-up: does the FY2026 proxy (next cycle) show a returns or margin metric added to the annual bonus?
- **Board independence-in-substance (true-independence concentration, no dedicated ID):** Is the Kilts/West/Ratzan Centerview Capital Consumer interlock, and their inherited SPAC-sponsor board-nomination right, a legally live mechanism today, or a lapsed legacy provision? Material to whether the "90.9% independent" figure overstates true board diversity of viewpoint. Follow-up: obtain confirmation of the nomination right's current legal status.
- **Shareholder-rights charter provisions (no dedicated ID):** Has the absence of special-meeting/written-consent rights ever been invoked to block a shareholder initiative? Not yet observed in the pool, but material if an activist or a hostile situation arises. Follow-up: monitor for any future proxy contest or activist campaign and how the board responds.

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **Stewardship verdict: Standard / mixed.** Strongest evidence for this read: the OWYN acquisition (71% impaired within 24 months, the clearest audited value-destruction event in the pool) sits alongside genuinely clean ownership/board mechanics (no controlling owner, zero pledging, ~0% related-party leakage) and candid disclosure of both major failures on verbatim transcripts.
- **Capital-allocation record: mixed, net value-destructive over the 5-year window scored, value-creative on the most recent evidence.** The FY2024 OWYN deal ($280.2M, debt-funded) destroyed value (71% impaired, ~0% incremental ROIC before the write-down); the FY2026 buyback ($213.2M at an $18.29 average, falling to $12.14) is genuinely per-share value-creative and corroborated by real insider buying at nearly the same price.
- **Incentive/ownership alignment: split.** The long-term PSU award is genuinely per-share (relative TSR); the annual cash bonus (paid every year) is 100% size-based; half the long-term award (RSUs) has no performance condition. Insider ownership is thin (8.76%, mostly a costless legacy stake) but recent insider buying (~$1.4M) is real and post-dates bad news, which is a governance positive on conduct even though the absolute skin-in-the-game level is modest.
- **Biggest governance risk / red flag:** RF-CAP-004 (serial-acquirer / OWYN value destruction) — Critical severity, caps capital allocation and floors governance risk. Second: RF-DISC-002 (recurring non-GAAP add-backs; Adjusted EBITDA structurally excludes a $391.9M recurring impairment) — also Critical severity, drags disclosure candor down from what would otherwise be a strong-band score.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan:** None. Verbatim: "No disqualifier triggered... All 8 hard disqualifiers are clean on the evidence in the data pool." The scan does flag two non-disqualifying items to carry forward: the $391.9M FY2026 Q3 combined goodwill/brand impairment and 31%/18% two-customer revenue concentration (Walmart/Amazon, 49% combined) — both already reflected in this module's scores and red flags.
- **§24 rejector filters tripped:** Filter 2 (turnaround without proven inflection) — Management quality capped at 60 (not the binding constraint; actual score 44). Filter 4 (serial acquirer) — Capital allocation capped at 50 (not binding; actual 47) and Governance risk floored at 60 (actual assigned 62). Filters 1 (integrity) and 6 (unaligned owner) — not triggered, both confirmed clean.
- **Partial-data caps applied:** None from the formal MODULE_RULES.md Partial-Data Rules table (`00_governance-data-triage` confirmed sufficiency with no caps triggered). The one real, dated gap: the current CEO's post-January-2026 compensation package and post-return ownership stake rest on web-sourced, unverified secondary reporting (confidence 1-2) because the primary SEC Form 8-K exhibits are not in the data pool — this discounts the Confidence Score but does not trigger a formal score cap.
- **Biggest missing data point (single highest-value next request):** The primary SEC Form 8-K Exhibits 10.1 (Scalzo employment agreement) and 10.2 (inducement option grant notice), filed 2026-01-21 — these would convert the new CEO's entire pay package and its performance conditions (or lack thereof) from web-sourced/unverified to a primary-filing-verified figure, and materially sharpen both the incentive-alignment and ownership reads going forward.
- **Explicit handoff:** This module supersedes the `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance` quick-read. The master synthesizer should treat this module's governance verdict (Standard / mixed) and scores (Governance Score 59, Confidence-Adjusted 50, Watchlist rating) as the primary governance read for SMPL.

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management has kept its routine guidance promises but broke the two that mattered most: the last guide before the CEO left, and the growth story behind a $280M acquisition that is now 71% written off.
- Capital allocation destroyed value on its biggest single decision (OWYN) but created real value on its most recent one (a large, price-disciplined 2026 stock buyback).
- The yearly cash bonus pays out on total sales and total profit dollars — not on profit per share or on returns — so it does not force management to think about value per share every year, even though the long-term stock award does.
- Insiders own a modest stake (8.76%, mostly inherited for free rather than bought), but the Chairman, CFO, a director, and a senior officer put about $1.4 million of their own cash into the stock in the last year, buying after bad news, not before it.
- Minority shareholders are protected from the obvious abuses — no pledged shares, no related-party sweetheart deals, one class of stock, no controlling family — but the board's independence is more technical than real at three seats, and shareholders can't call a special meeting or act without one.
- Management has been unusually blunt about its own mistakes on earnings calls, but the company's main "clean" profit number keeps excluding a nearly $400 million pattern of write-downs that has now shown up three times in a year.
- No hard governance disqualifier was found — no audit problem, no pledged shares, no fraud, no related-party leakage, no restatement.
- This module is useful for the master synthesizer: it is data-sufficient, covers all six required lenses, and should be read as the primary governance verdict for SMPL, replacing the quick-read.

RF-CAP-004,"Serial-acquirer pattern; OWYN acquisition ~$280.2M debt-funded 71% impaired within 24 months, incremental ROIC ~0% before write-down",Critical,"FY26 Q3 10-Q Note 4; FY2025 10-K p.43","FY26 Q3 10-Q, filed 2026-07-09","Capital allocation capped at 50 (actual 47, not binding); Governance risk floored at 60 (actual set at 62)","Monitor for a third material deal before OWYN stabilizes"
RF-DISC-002,"Recurring one-off non-GAAP add-backs; Adjusted EBITDA excludes $391.9M impairment pattern recurring 3 of last 4 quarters; adjustments 56.3% of GAAP EBITDA FY2025",Critical,"earnings/06_earnings-quality.md sec4,sec9,sec10; FY2025 10-K","FY2025 10-K filed 2025-10-28; FQ3 FY26 10-Q filed 2026-07-09","Disclosure candor dragged to 75 (lower half of strong band)","Track normalization; escalate if a 4th impairment occurs"
RF-MGT-004,"Turnaround claimed without proven inflection - zero years delivered, every metric still declining YoY",High,"Q2/Q3 FY26 transcripts; earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md sec6","Q3 FY26 earnings call, 2026-07-09","Management quality capped at 60 (actual 44, not binding); conviction cap","Track whether YoY declines narrow to real growth"
NONE-01,"Three senior leadership changes (CEO, CFO predecessor, Controller/CAO) in 12 months coincident with largest-ever impairment",High,"CIQ Key Developments.rtf entries 2026-01-19/20/21/28; 2026 Proxy p.60-61","CIQ Key Developments, data as of 2026-07-24","Contributes to Governance risk 62 assignment; cross-ref business-model/11 severity 55","Track finance/leadership bench stabilization"
NONE-02,"Departing CEO Tanner's final guidance print missed by -5.1% to -6.1%, issued 10 days before departure",High,"Estimates Report.xls Guidance tab; Q2 FY26 earnings call","Q2 FY26 earnings call, 2026-04-09","Contributes to Management quality score (-12 penalty)","Was the guide built on already-deteriorating data?"
NONE-03,"CEO severance $3.465M-$5.2M for the executive responsible for the OWYN deal, under a CIC multiplier raised 2.0x to 3.0x months earlier",High,"2026 Proxy p.60","2026 Proxy, mailed 2025-12-17","Contributes to Management quality score (-4 penalty); cross-ref business-model/12 severity 50","None - fully disclosed"
RF-MGT-003,"Annual cash bonus 100% size-based (net sales 50% + absolute Adjusted EBITDA 50%), no per-share or returns metric",High,"2026 Proxy p.44-45","2026 Proxy, mailed 2025-12-17","Incentive alignment score reduced (-10 penalty)","Watch for a returns/margin metric added under new CEO"
NONE-04,"Charter/bylaw stack denies shareholders the right to call a special meeting or act by written consent",High,"FY2025 10-K, Anti-takeover provisions, Risk Factors","FY2025 10-K, filed 2025-10-28","Shareholder-rights component score reduced (-12 penalty)","Has this ever blocked a shareholder proposal?"

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Sufficient
- **Reason:** The pool contains a current proxy statement (compensation, beneficial ownership, board composition/independence, and related-party disclosure), a Form-4-sourced insider-transaction export, a full audited FY2025 10-K, two recent 10-Qs, two recent earnings-call transcripts, and multi-year capital-allocation history — meeting all three legs of the Sufficient rule (proxy/comp AND ownership AND board/related-party, plus multi-year capital-allocation history).
- **Specialists that can run:** management track record, capital allocation, incentives, ownership, board, candor — all six.
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — the scan found no triggered disqualifier (unqualified Deloitte & Touche audit opinion, no going-concern note, no promoter/insider pledging >50%, single-class common stock with no controlling group). It does flag two non-disqualifying items worth carrying forward: a combined $391.9mm FY2026 Q3 goodwill/brand impairment (OWYN and Atkins, tied to sustained share-price decline) and 31%/18% two-customer revenue concentration (Walmart/Amazon, 49% combined).
- **Active partial-data caps:** None — no score caps from MODULE_RULES.md are triggered.
- **Critical missing items:** None. Minor note: no standalone shareholder letter exists for this issuer (not a pool gap — transcripts and 10-K MD&A substitute); 2026 AGM voting-results outcome not separately confirmed in this triage read (prior-year 2025 results are disclosed in the proxy).
- **Single highest-value missing document:** None required for sufficiency. If available, the 2026 AGM voting-results 8-K (or equivalent post-meeting filing) would sharpen the shareholder-rights read (votes-against on remuneration/RPT/director elections) but is not needed to clear the sufficiency bar.

# Management & Track Record — SMPL

## 1. Key Executives

| Role | Name | Appointed | Tenure | Relevant Background | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CEO (President & CEO) | Joseph E. Scalzo | CEO effective Jan 19, 2026 (returning); appointed to Board Jan 28, 2026 | ~6.5 months in this stint as of report date (2026-08-06). Previously CEO of Simply Good Foods / predecessor Atkins Nutritionals for a "decade-long tenure" through Jul 2023, then Executive Vice Chairman until Aug 2024, then fully off the board/company Aug 2024–Jan 2026 (~17-month gap) | Led the company's 2017 IPO (via the Conyers Park SPAC combination) and the Nov-2019 Quest Nutrition acquisition/integration as prior CEO. Earlier: senior roles at Dean Foods (incl. President/COO), CEO of WhiteWave Foods, and executive roles at Gillette and Coca-Cola. Age 66. Currently also sits on the boards of Freshpet and TreeHouse Foods | [CIQ Key Developments.rtf, entries dated Jan-20-2026 ("Announces Chief Executive Officer Changes")] |
| CFO | Christopher J. Bealer | CFO effective Jul 3, 2025 (SVP Finance from Apr 1, 2025) | ~13 months total with the company; ~13 months as CFO. Since Feb 6, 2026 also holds the Principal Accounting Officer title (see §2) | Outside hire with no prior SMPL history. ~23 years in CPG/consumer durables: EVP Corporate Controller and CFO Health at Reckitt Benckiser, VP/CFO roles (EMEA, North America) at Whirlpool, earlier finance roles at Big Heart Pet Brands, Del Monte Foods, H.J. Heinz. US CPA and UK Associate Chartered Accountant | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Executive Officers," p.35–36] |
| Chairman of the Board | James M. Kilts | Chairman since Jul 2017; director since 2017 | ~9 years as Chairman | Founding Partner of Centerview Capital Consumer (2006). Former Chairman/CEO/President of Gillette (2001–2005; oversaw ~$30bn of equity-value creation cited in his own bio) and former President/CEO of Nabisco (1998–2000). Age 77. Largest individual shareholder (4.27%); bought 80,000 shares in the open market in Apr-2026 near the stock's lows | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Election of Directors," p.27] |

**Founder / owner-operator status:** SMPL has no founder-CEO and no controlling family or promoter group — it was formed via a 2017 SPAC combination (Conyers Park Acquisition Corp + Atkins Nutritionals) and is a widely-held company (insiders/officers/directors hold 9.30% of shares in aggregate) [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan §1, item 2; CIQ Public Ownership Summary.rtf]. Scalzo is the closest analogue to a builder-operator — he ran the business (as CEO of the predecessor and then SMPL) through the 2017 IPO and the 2019 Quest acquisition — but he is a hired executive, not an owner-founder, and his direct shareholding is not disclosed at a level comparable to a founder stake (ownership detail is agent 04's domain).

**Prior leadership no longer in place (context for §2):** Geoff E. Tanner — President and CEO Jul 7, 2023 to Jan 18, 2026 (President/COO/CEO-elect from Apr 3, 2023); previously Chief Commercial and Marketing Officer at J.M. Smucker (2019–2023) and roles at Big Heart Pet Brands/Del Monte Foods (2003–2016) [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Election of Directors," p.33]. Shaun P. Mara — CFO through his retirement effective Jul 3, 2025 [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Summary Compensation Table" notes, p.60–61]. Timothy A. Matthews — VP, Controller and Chief Accounting Officer since Jul 2017 (Atkins predecessor role from Nov 2016), resigned Jan 20, 2026 effective Feb 6, 2026 to take an external CFO role [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Executive Officers," p.35; CIQ Key Developments.rtf, entry Jan-21-2026].

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 years) | Three finance/CEO leadership changes inside roughly 12 months: CEO Geoff Tanner departed all positions Jan 18, 2026; CFO Shaun Mara retired Jul 3, 2025 (planned, orderly handoff to Bealer, same-day); Controller/Chief Accounting Officer Timothy Matthews resigned Jan 20, 2026 (effective Feb 6, 2026) to take an external CFO job. A Chief Human Resources Officer departure (Amy Held, exiting by Jun 1, 2026) and a Chief Supply Chain Officer separation (Jason Bendure) followed as part of a "modified organization design" restructuring announced under the returning CEO | [CIQ Key Developments.rtf, entries Jan-19/20/21/28-2026, and the "cost savings and realignment initiatives" entry]; [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, comp-table footnotes, p.60–61] |
| CFO turnover | One CFO transition in the trailing 12 months (Mara → Bealer, Jul 2025), described as a retirement with a same-day handoff — not evidence of instability on its own. More material: since Feb 6, 2026 Bealer holds BOTH the CFO (principal financial officer) and Principal Accounting Officer titles after Matthews' resignation, concentrating the two senior finance-oversight roles in one person who had been CFO for only seven months at that point. No material weakness is disclosed (FQ3 FY26 10-Q states disclosure controls were "effective"), but this is a thinning of the finance bench during the same fiscal year as a $391.9M combined goodwill/intangible impairment and its associated critical-audit-matter judgment calls — a genuine, if not yet proven, control-depth concern | [CIQ Public Ownership Insider Trading.xls, "Bealer, Christopher James (CFO & Principal Accounting Officer)"]; [FQ3 FY26 10-Q, Item 4 (Controls and Procedures)]; cross-ref [business-model/12_red-flags-sweep, "CFO now also serves as sole Principal Accounting Officer," severity 35] |
| CEO succession / continuity | Not an internal-bench succession — the Board went outside the current executive team and reached back to a previous CEO who had already left the company once (departed the CEO role Jul 2023, left entirely Aug 2024) to replace Tanner on ten days' notice from his own last guidance print. This is evidence of thin internal CEO-succession depth, not proof of a deep bench | [CIQ Key Developments.rtf, Jan-20-2026 entry]; Inference, not from filings: the ~17-month gap in Scalzo's SMPL involvement and the choice of a "known quantity" over an internal successor is consistent with a board that judged its internal bench insufficiently ready |

**Warning flagged.** Three senior leadership changes (CEO, CFO's predecessor, Controller/CAO) inside a single 12-month window, landing in the same window as the company's largest-ever non-cash impairment ($391.9M) and a CEO exit tied to the value-destroying OWYN acquisition, is a material stability concern. This is not presented as a fresh finding — it duplicates and is scored by `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance` (severity 55/100, inverted) and `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep` (CEO severance economics, severity 50; CFO/PAO dual-hat, severity 35) — this report references those findings for the management-competence read rather than re-scoring them.

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

Under the returning CEO, management has framed FY2026 as the early stage of an explicit, three-part turnaround: (1) "strengthening the economics of the business" through cost discipline and pricing (a high-single-digit price increase across most of the portfolio effective September 2026), (2) "consistency and discipline in strategic choices," and (3) "rebuilding brand investment behind superior consumer insights and marketing execution" [Q3 FY26 earnings call, Jul 9, 2026, prepared remarks (CEO Scalzo)]. Management has also stated an explicit, checkable long-run financial target — its own description of what the P&L looked like when the business was healthy: gross margin "approaching 40%," marketing spend "around 10% of sales," and Adjusted EBITDA margin "approaching 20%" — versus a current structure of gross margin in the "middle 30s" and a compressed marketing ratio [Q2 FY26 earnings call, Apr 9, 2026, prepared remarks (CEO Scalzo); reaffirmed as the "long-term algorithm" in the Q3 FY26 call, Jul 9, 2026]. No date is attached to when these targets should be reached — CEO Scalzo explicitly declined to give a turnaround timeline on the Q3 call ("I caution against thinking about turnaround in terms of a time...The when, it's going to come in stages...My crystal ball is not that good") [Q3 FY26 earnings call, Jul 9, 2026, Q&A].

## 4. Promise vs Delivery

Guidance history is available back to FY2018 in the Capital IQ Guidance tab, cross-checked against the two FY2026 earnings-call transcripts and the FY2025 10-K — this is a checkable, multi-year promise-vs-delivery record, not a data-starved read.

| Prior Promise / Target | Period Set | Actual Outcome | Kept / Missed | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY2023 revenue guidance $1,238.8M (issued Jun 29, 2023, at the Scalzo→Tanner CEO transition) | FY2023 | Actual $1,242.7M | Kept (+0.3%) | [CIQ Estimates Report.xls, Guidance tab] |
| FY2023 Adjusted EBITDA guidance $248.1M | FY2023 | Actual $245.6M | Missed (-1.0%) | [CIQ Estimates Report.xls, Guidance tab] |
| FY2024 revenue guidance $1,304.8M (issued Jun 27, 2024, Tanner, mid-OWYN-acquisition period) | FY2024 | Actual $1,331.3M | Kept / beat (+2.0%) | [CIQ Estimates Report.xls, Guidance tab] |
| FY2024 Adjusted EBITDA guidance $265.2M | FY2024 | Actual $269.1M | Kept / beat (+1.5%) | [CIQ Estimates Report.xls, Guidance tab] |
| FY2025 revenue guidance $1,444.5M–$1,457.8M (issued Jul 10, 2025, Tanner) | FY2025 | Actual $1,450.9M | Kept (in-range) | [CIQ Estimates Report.xls, Guidance tab; Annual Report on Form 10-K_2025] |
| FY2025 Adjusted EBITDA guidance $279.9M–$282.6M (issued Jul 10, 2025, Tanner) | FY2025 | Actual $278.2M | Missed (narrowly, -0.6% below the guided floor) | [CIQ Estimates Report.xls, Guidance tab; Annual Report on Form 10-K_2025] |
| **FQ2 FY2026 net-sales guidance $343.5M–$347.1M — Tanner's last guidance print, issued Jan 8, 2026, ten days before his departure** | FQ2 FY2026 (quarter ended Feb 28, 2026) | Actual $326.0M | **Missed badly (-5.1% to -6.1% below the guided range)** — management's own words on the next call: "well below our expectations" | [CIQ Estimates Report.xls, Guidance tab; Q2 FY26 earnings call, Apr 9, 2026, prepared remarks (CEO Scalzo)] |
| OWYN acquisition (Jun 2024, ~$280.4M, Tanner-era decision, Board-approved) — implicit promise that the deal would add a durable, accretive growth platform | ~24 months post-close | $200.0M of the ~$280.4M purchase price (71%) taken as an intangible/brand impairment by FQ3 FY2026, alongside a $93.0M impairment of the founding Atkins brand and $38.0M of goodwill in the same quarter | **Missed — value destroyed, not created** | [FY26 Q3 10-Q, Note 4 (Goodwill and Intangibles)]; cross-ref [business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance §1] |
| FY2026 full-year guidance $1.31B–$1.35B net sales / $217M–$225M Adjusted EBITDA (issued Apr 9, 2026 — Scalzo's first guidance print after returning) | FY2026 | Narrowed and raised the low end to $1.345B–$1.355B net sales / $220M–$225M EBITDA by Jul 9, 2026, on three consecutive quarterly beats (revenue +0.1%→+0.3%→+7.3%; EPS +8.1%→+13.6%→+19.5%) | Kept / exceeded own guide so far — in progress (FQ4 FY2026 not yet reported as of this report's date) | [Q2 FY26 and Q3 FY26 earnings-call transcripts; `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` §6] |
| "Long-term algorithm" targets (~40% gross margin, ~10% marketing/sales, ~20% Adjusted EBITDA margin), first stated Apr 9, 2026, reaffirmed Jul 9, 2026 | Multi-year, no date attached | FY2026 is guided to a GAAP gross margin of roughly 32.5% and an implied Adjusted EBITDA margin of roughly 16.3–16.7% at the guided midpoint — well below the stated targets | Too early to grade — a stated target, not yet a promise with a delivery date | [Q2 FY26 and Q3 FY26 earnings-call transcripts] |

**Read:** The record is genuinely mixed, and the two most consequential broken promises are the worst ones. FY2023–FY2025 guidance was kept or only narrowly missed on the headline lines — unremarkable, normal-course accuracy. But the two promises that actually mattered — a $280.4M acquisition pitched as a growth platform, and the last quarterly guide the departing CEO gave before his own exit — were both missed badly, and both landed in the same window as the CEO's departure. The three-quarter beat streak since the CEO change (Apr–Jul 2026) is real and cited, but it is short, follows a steep guidance reset (FY2026 guidance was cut from what was implicit in FY2025's trajectory to $1.31B–$1.35B), and coexists with genuinely negative FY2027 Street revision breadth (-4 to -6 across revenue/EBITDA/EPS in the last three months) [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` §5] — the market has not yet been convinced the reset guide itself is conservative rather than merely lower.

## 4A. Turnaround & Integrity Tests (CLAUDE.md §24, Filters 1 & 2)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Is the thesis a turnaround (struggling business + new management / new plan)? | **Yes, explicitly.** Management itself uses the word "turnaround" repeatedly and states "we remain in the early stages of our turnaround" with "significant work ahead" [Q3 FY26 earnings call, Jul 9, 2026]. Net sales, gross margin, and Adjusted EBITDA are all declining year-over-year through FQ3 FY2026, and FY2026 guidance itself implies a further decline (net sales -7% to -6%, Adjusted EBITDA -21% to -19%) | [Q2/Q3 FY26 transcripts; `04_guidance-consensus.md` §2] |
| If yes: years of *proven* operating inflection actually delivered (not promised) | **Zero.** The "new" leadership has been in place for two reported quarters (FQ2 and FQ3 FY2026, Feb–May 2026). Both quarters beat a recently-lowered guide, but both quarters still show net sales, gross margin, and Adjusted EBITDA declining year-over-year (net sales -9.4% then -6.3%; gross margin -460bps then -390bps; Adjusted EBITDA -18.4% then -22.5%) [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` §6; Q2/Q3 FY26 transcripts]. Two quarters of narrowing declines against a lowered bar is not the §24 Filter 2 standard of "2–3 years of *proven* operating improvement" | [Q2/Q3 FY26 earnings-call transcripts] |
| Résumé / plan / consultant deck cited as the reason to believe? (not evidence) | **Yes, directly.** The company's own public messaging leans on Scalzo's résumé as the reason to believe the turnaround will work: he "presided over a period of substantial value creation" and brings "immediate familiarity of the Company, its brands and infrastructure" [CIQ Key Developments.rtf, Jan-20-2026 entry]. Chairman Kilts' own bio is built the same way — his Gillette tenure is credited with "$30 billion in equity value" and cited by Harvard Business Review as a "turnaround" [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, p.27]. This is exactly the résumé-as-evidence pattern §24 Filter 2 warns against: a credentialed operator's plan is not proof of inflection until the numbers show it | [CIQ Key Developments.rtf; 2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, p.27] |
| Routed integrity "buzz" from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (unverified adverse signal) | **None routed.** The disqualifier scan found all eight hard disqualifiers clean (no audit qualification, no pledging, no material related-party leakage, no auditor-change pattern, no restatement, no enforcement action, no >40% single-customer concentration, no negative-operating-cash-flow pattern) and did not flag any unverified adverse integrity signal for this agent to chase [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan §3]. No `RF-MGT-005` is warranted | [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md] |

**Turnaround rule applied.** Per CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 2 and the MODULE_RULES score-cap table, a turnaround thesis without ≥2–3 years of delivered operating inflection caps Management Quality at a maximum of 60/100 and the thesis is classified as a **governance-turnaround**, not a proven compounder. This report's own Management Quality Score (§ below) already lands below that cap on the underlying evidence, so the cap is not the binding constraint here — but the base-rate penalty is: a star-CEO turnaround pitch, on its own, is not proof of inflection, and the base rate of turnaround success is low. Conviction on this name should be capped accordingly until at least several more quarters of genuinely improving (not merely less-bad, or beating a lowered bar) year-over-year numbers are visible.

## 5. Management Read

Six months into his second stint, Scalzo and a mostly-new finance function (a 13-month CFO now also doubling as principal accounting officer) are running a business whose most recent proven leadership team — the one that acquired OWYN and set the last guidance print before its own exit — left behind a $391.9M impairment and a guidance miss of over 5% on its final quarterly call. The two quarters since the change have beaten a lowered bar and management has been candid about the shortfall in blunt terms ("not satisfied," "well below our expectations"), which is a genuine positive, but candor about a miss is not the same as proof the turnaround is working: every headline operating metric is still declining year-over-year, and the résumé-driven case for confidence ("substantial value creation," a "decade-long tenure") is exactly the kind of evidence CLAUDE.md §24 says is not evidence. The single biggest management signal is the OWYN acquisition: a $280.4M, debt-funded, board-approved bet that was 71% written off within two years, followed by the CEO who did the deal collecting a $3.465M–$5.2M severance package under a Change-in-Control multiplier the Board had raised just months earlier [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Potential Payments Upon Termination or Change in Control," p.60; cross-ref business-model/12_red-flags-sweep].

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — SMPL

Reporting currency: USD (millions, unless per-share). Fiscal year ends the last Saturday in August; FY2024 was a 53-week year. US GAAP. Source: Simply Good Foods (SMPL), Nasdaq-listed, no controlling promoter [FY2025 10-K; 2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement].

## 1. Uses of Capital (3–5 years)

Primary window: FY2021–FY2025 (five full fiscal years, Aug-28-2021 to Aug-30-2025). A separate LTM line (FY2026 through May-30-2026, ~9 months) is shown because it captures a capital-allocation shift material enough to change the read — buybacks alone in that partial year exceed the entire five-year total.

| Use | Cumulative Amount, FY21–FY25 | % of CFO (FY21–25 CFO = $808.0M) | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Acquisitions (net) | $280.2M (all FY2024, OWYN; a $1.7M escrow release in FY2025 reduced net consideration to $280.2M) | 34.7% | [FY2025 10-K, p.43, MD&A — "total net consideration paid of $280.2 million"] |
| Buybacks | $131.25M ($16.45M FY23 + $0 FY24 + $50.9M FY25, all from the 10-K, + $0.4M FY21 + $63.5M FY22 from the CIQ annual export — the two oldest years are not corroborated by a filing in this pool and are flagged lower-confidence) | 16.2% | [FY2025 10-K, p.73–74, Note 12; CIQ Financials_Annual.xls, Cash Flow tab] |
| Dividends | $0 — no dividend ever paid | 0.0% | [FY2025 10-K, p.32, "Dividends"] |
| Capex | $48.9M ($5.9M + $5.2M + $11.6M + $5.7M + $20.5M) | 6.1% | [earnings/01_historical-financials.md §1; CIQ Financials_Annual.xls, Cash Flow tab] |
| Debt repayment (net of $250M FY2024 issuance) | $357.5M ($607.5M gross repaid − $250M issued for OWYN) | 44.2% | [CIQ Financials_Annual.xls, Cash Flow tab: Long-Term Debt Repaid/Issued] |

Rows sum to ~101% of five-year CFO ($818.4M of $808.0M) — the small overshoot is funded by option-exercise proceeds ($27.5M cumulative) and a modest net cash drawdown, both immaterial. FCF (CFO − capex) over the same five years was $759.1M [earnings/01_historical-financials.md §1].

**LTM (FY2026 through May-30-2026, 9 months) — the standout event.** Buybacks alone were $213.2M in 39 weeks (11,651,767 shares at an average $18.29/share, inclusive of commissions, exclusive of excise tax), funded partly by cash on hand and partly by a fresh $150.0M long-term debt issuance in the same period — i.e., the company borrowed to buy back stock while GAAP earnings were negative (a $391.9M non-cash impairment) [10-Q Jul-09-2026, p.31–32, MD&A "Liquidity and Capital Resources"; p.15, Note (Stockholders' Equity)]. This single 9-month period moved more capital through buybacks than the prior five full fiscal years combined ($213.2M vs $131.25M) — see §3.

## 2. M&A Scorecard

| Deal | Year | Price | Disclosed Rationale / Synergy | Delivered? | Opportunity Cost (divestitures forced / focus lost / options foregone) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Quest Nutrition (pre-window context — closed Nov-2019, outside the 3–5yr scorecard but the necessary base-rate comparator for the serial-acquirer test below) | FY2020 | ~$982.1M cash | Entry into the high-protein bar/snack category alongside Atkins | Partially provable: net sales grew from "$350 million when we acquired it" to "$863 million" in FY2025 (2.5x) [FY2025 10-K, p.43, MD&A]. No brand-level profit/ROI is disclosed — SMPL reports one GAAP segment [FY2025 10-K, Note 15] — so the *revenue* growth is verified but the *profit* return on the $982.1M is not independently disclosed or computable from this pool. | No divestiture forced; no evidence of opportunity cost beyond the debt load taken on at the time (debt jumped from $190.9M FY19 to $624.2M FY20). | [FY2025 10-K, p.43; CIQ Financials_Annual.xls, Cash Flow tab] |
| OWYN (Only What You Need) | Jun-2024 (FY2024) | $280.2M net consideration, funded by a $250.0M incremental Term Facility borrowing plus cash on hand | An "accretive" growth-platform addition in ready-to-drink protein, per deal announcement framing (not independently quoted in this pool beyond the funding/price mechanics) | **No — value destroyed, not created.** $200.0M of the $280.4M gross purchase price (71%) was taken as an intangible/brand impairment inside the 39 weeks ended May-30-2026, alongside a separate $38.0M goodwill impairment and a $93.0M impairment of the (unrelated, organically-built) Atkins brand in the same quarter [FY26 Q3 10-Q, Note 4]. The Board's own goodwill/intangible test cites "a sustained decline in the Company's share price and declines in the Company's market capitalization" as the impairment trigger [FY26 Q3 10-Q, Note 4] — the market had already marked the deal down before the accounting caught up. | Debt-funded: the $250.0M term-loan draw pushed total debt from $326.6M (FY23) to $437.3M (FY24), a 34% jump, at the same company that then had to draw a fresh $150.0M of debt in FY2026 to keep buying back stock while leverage was already rising into a downturn. Focus lost: OWYN integration (FY2024–FY2025) coincided with base-business margin erosion (gross margin −220bps FY25, Adjusted EBITDA margin −330bps LTM [earnings/01_historical-financials.md §2]) and preceded a wave of C-suite exits (CEO, CFO's predecessor, Controller/CAO) within ~18 months of close. Options foregone: the $280.2M spent on OWYN in mid-2024, when SMPL's own shares traded in the low-to-mid $30s (the FY2025 buyback price disclosed for that period was $31.95–$35.10 [FY2025 10-K, p.73; FY26 Q3 10-Q, p.15]), could instead have retired roughly 8–9M shares (~8–9% of the then ~99.6M shares outstanding) at those prices — instead the company later had to buy back stock at $12–19 only after its own M&A decision had helped drive the price down. | [FY26 Q3 10-Q, Note 4 (Goodwill and Intangibles); FY2025 10-K, p.43; cross-ref `01_management-and-track-record.md` §4 finding 01-006] |

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).** SMPL is a serial acquirer by the plain reading of the record: two material, debt-funded deals in six years (Quest 2019, ~$982.1M; OWYN 2024, ~$280.2M), the more recent of which is 71% impaired within 24 months, with the impairment explicitly triggered by the market's own repricing of the stock. Bare "accretive growth platform" language at the time of the OWYN deal is not evidence of value creation — it was not delivered. Per the MODULE_RULES score-cap table this triggers **RF-CAP-004** and caps the Capital Allocation Score at **50/100** regardless of the cleaner signals elsewhere in this scorecard (low historical leverage, no dividend cut, insider buying). This is the single most material capital-allocation signal in this report.

## 3. Buyback Scorecard

Intrinsic-value discipline is not assessable from this module — no valuation module has run for this ticker as of this report. Judgment below is limited to observable evidence: net share-count change, buyback price relative to the company's own historical trading/repurchase range, whether buybacks occurred while leverage rose, whether they merely offset stock-based-comp (SBC) dilution, and corroborating insider-purchase evidence.

| Period | Amount Spent | Shares Bought | Implied Avg Price | Share-Count Change (period) | Offset SBC Dilution? | Price-Discipline Evidence | Judgment | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| FY2021 | $0.4M | Not disclosed at share-level in this pool (CIQ dollar figure only; not corroborated by a filing) | Not determinable from available data | Shares outstanding +0.14% (95.65M→95.78M) | **No** — dilution exceeded buyback | No price data | Trivial repurchase; dilution from RSU/PSU issuance dominated | CIQ Financials_Annual.xls (flagged: no filing corroboration for this year in this pool) |
| FY2022 | $63.5M | Not disclosed at share-level in this pool | Not determinable | Shares outstanding +3.9% (95.78M→99.50M) | **No** — dilution (incl. an acquisition-linked equity raise) exceeded buyback | No price data | Buyback failed to prevent meaningful net dilution | CIQ Financials_Annual.xls (flagged: no filing corroboration for this year in this pool) |
| FY2023 | $16.45M | 546,346 shares | $30.11 | Shares outstanding +0.06% (99.50M→99.56M) | **Roughly** — buyback approximately offset the year's dilution, net count nearly flat | Bought at a mid-range historical price; no distress signal | Modest, disciplined-scale repurchase; kept share count flat | [FY2025 10-K, p.73–74, Note 12] |
| FY2024 | $0 | 0 — "did not repurchase any shares of common stock" | N/A | Shares outstanding +0.59% (99.56M→100.15M) | **No** — capital instead went to the $250M-funded OWYN deal | N/A | Capital allocated to M&A instead of buybacks in the same year the deal proved value-destructive (§2) | [FY2025 10-K, p.43–44] |
| FY2025 | $50.9M | 1,592,471 shares | $31.95 (blended: 693,375 sh @ $35.10 in the 39 weeks to May-2025, ~899,096 sh @ ~$29.6 implied in Q4 FY2025) | Shares outstanding −0.42% (100.15M→99.73M) | **Yes, marginally** — first year of net share-count reduction | Bought in a $30–35 range across most of the year — no evidence of buying into a price spike or a trough | First genuine net reduction, but at prices well above where the stock ultimately traded 6–9 months later | [FY2025 10-K, p.43–44, 73–74] |
| FQ1–FQ2 FY2026 (Sep-2025–Feb-2026, ~26wk) | ~$188.2M (derived: $213.2M 39wk total − $25.0M FQ3 alone) | ~9,590,504 shares (derived: 11,651,767 − 2,061,263) | ~$19.62 (derived) | Not separately disclosed at this granularity | Directionally yes | Buying accelerated sharply as the price fell through the CEO/guidance-miss period; Board doubled the authorization by $200.0M on Jan-6-2026 | Aggressive scale-up into a falling price — good directional discipline on price, but overlaps the same window as the leadership churn and a fresh debt draw | [FY26 Q3 10-Q, p.15, 31 — derived from disclosed 13wk/39wk totals] |
| FQ3 FY2026 (Mar–May-2026, 13wk) | $25.0M (2,061,263 sh × $12.14) | 2,061,263 shares | $12.14 | Not separately disclosed at this granularity | Directionally yes | Bought near the stock's multi-year lows; **converges with independent insider buying** — Chairman Kilts bought 80,000 shares at $12.39 (Apr-23-2026) and director Daley bought 10,000 shares at $11.78 (May-14-2026), both open-market [cross-ref `01_management-and-track-record.md` §5; `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1] | The single best-evidenced price-discipline data point in this scorecard: company buyback price and two separate insiders' own purchase prices landed within $0.36 of each other in the same six-week window | [FY26 Q3 10-Q, p.15] |
| **FY2026 YTD total (39wk to May-30-2026)** | **$213.2M** | **11,651,767 shares** | **$18.29** | **Shares outstanding −11.3% (99.73M FY25 → 88.44M May-2026)** | **Yes, decisively** — this is the first period where buybacks clearly outpaced all dilution | Average price ($18.29) sits well below every FY2023–FY2025 repurchase price ($30–35) — the company bought more, not less, as the price fell | The one genuinely strong capital-allocation action in the period, but funded partly by $150.0M of fresh debt drawn in the same 39 weeks, while GAAP earnings were negative from the OWYN/Atkins impairment — a leverage-into-a-downturn trade-off (cross-ref balance-sheet-survival module for solvency-level judgment) | [FY26 Q3 10-Q, p.15, 31–32] |

**Read on the buyback record.** FY2021–FY2024 buybacks were small and did not prevent net share-count dilution in any of those four years — RSU/PSU issuance consistently outran repurchases. FY2025 was the inflection point (first net reduction, though still bought at $30–35). The FY2026 acceleration is the real story: $213.2M spent in 39 weeks at an average $18.29, with the most recent quarter's $12.14 average price corroborated by two insiders buying their own shares within 36 cents of that price in the same window. That is genuine, evidence-backed price discipline on an observable basis. The complication is financing: the buyback surge coincided with a fresh $150.0M debt draw and a GAAP net loss, so part of the "buy low" story was funded by releveraging into a period of declining Adjusted EBITDA, not purely out of retained free cash flow.

## 4. Dividends & Organic Reinvestment

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| Payout ratio | 0% — no dividend has ever been paid; company states it "does not expect to declare any dividends in the foreseeable future" | [FY2025 10-K, p.32, "Dividends"] |
| Dividend coverage (FCF) | Not applicable — no dividend to cover | [FY2025 10-K, p.32] |
| Incremental ROIC, FY2022 (ΔNOPAT / Δinvested capital, invested capital = total debt + total equity − cash) | **+26.6%** (ΔNOPAT $56.3M / ΔIC $211.5M) | Inference, not from filings — computed by this agent from GAAP EBIT [earnings/01_historical-financials.md §1] and CIQ effective tax rates [CIQ Financials_Annual.xls, Income Statement tab] and balance-sheet debt/equity/cash [CIQ Financials_Annual.xls, Balance Sheet tab] |
| Incremental ROIC, FY2023 | Not meaningful — invested capital *contracted* $15.2M (net debt paydown outpaced retained-earnings growth) while NOPAT rose modestly; the ratio is not interpretable as a return figure | Inference, not from filings — same method as above |
| Incremental ROIC, FY2024 (the OWYN acquisition year) | **−0.5%** (ΔNOPAT −$1.2M / ΔIC +$222.3M) | Inference, not from filings — same method as above. This is the cleanest quantitative confirmation available in this pool that the capital deployed in FY2024 (dominated by the $250M OWYN-related borrowing) did not generate incremental operating profit, consistent with the subsequent 71% impairment (§2) |
| Incremental ROIC, FY2025 | Not meaningful — invested capital contracted $19.5M (debt paydown) while NOPAT fell $35.0M, itself partly a mechanical effect of the FY2025 $60.9M Atkins impairment sitting inside GAAP EBIT; the two moves are not causally linked in a way a ratio can capture | Inference, not from filings — same method as above |

**Organic reinvestment read.** Capex is small and stable ($5.2M–$20.5M/year, 0.4%–1.5% of revenue), consistent with a co-manufactured, asset-light model rather than aggressive self-funded capacity build [cross-ref `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` §1, capex-intensity row]. The one clean organic-period data point (FY2022, post-Quest integration, no M&A that year) shows strong incremental returns on capital (+26.6%). The one M&A-dominated data point (FY2024, the OWYN year) shows the capital deployed earned an incremental return close to zero, before the subsequent write-down. No brand-level profit is disclosed (SMPL reports one GAAP segment), so a cleaner organic-vs-acquired capital-efficiency split is **not proven from available data** beyond these two computed data points.

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

Capital allocation over FY2021–FY2026 YTD has been genuinely mixed, and the two extremes are stark: the FY2024 OWYN acquisition — a $280.2M, debt-funded bet dressed as an accretive growth platform — has already had 71% of its purchase price written off within two years, with the company's own goodwill test citing the market's own price decline as the trigger, and the incremental capital it consumed earned roughly a 0% return before the write-down (§4). Against that, the FY2026 buyback program is the best-evidenced positive action in this scorecard: $213.2M spent at an average $18.29, accelerating to $12.14 in the most recent quarter, corroborated by two insiders buying their own stock at nearly the same price in the same window — genuine, observable price discipline, though partly funded by a fresh $150.0M debt draw rather than pure free cash flow. Best use of capital: the FY2026 buyback surge (net share count actually fell 11.3% in nine months, the first period in the five-plus years reviewed where repurchases decisively outran dilution). Worst use of capital: the OWYN acquisition, both on its own terms (71% impaired) and on an opportunity-cost basis (the $280.2M spent in mid-2024 near $30–35/share could have retired ~8–9% of the share count at a price the market has not seen since). No dividend has ever been paid, which is a deliberate policy, not a distress signal, but it means all of the company's capital-return discipline runs through buyback timing alone — and that timing has been inconsistent (small, dilution-losing purchases at $30+ for years, followed by a large, debt-assisted purchase at $12–19 only after a self-inflicted write-down had already cratered the price). **Verdict: mixed** — value-creative on the recent buyback evidence, value-destructive on the M&A record that dominates the five-year capital total, with the serial-acquirer pattern (RF-CAP-004) capping the overall score regardless of the buyback improvement.

# Incentives & Compensation — SMPL

## 1. Compensation Structure

Reporting currency: USD. Period: fiscal year 2025 (ended August 30, 2025) is the most recently disclosed full pay-package year in the 2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement (the "2025 Incentive Plan" / "FY2025 LTI Program"). The company's Principal Executive Officer ("PEO") changed mid-cycle: Geoff E. Tanner was CEO through the FY2025 disclosure period and departed January 18, 2026; Joseph E. Scalzo (a returning former CEO) became President and CEO effective January 19, 2026, under a newly-disclosed employment agreement that is referenced but not reproduced in the data pool.

| Executive | Base | Annual Bonus (target / actual FY2025) | Long-Term Incentive | Cash vs Equity Mix (target, CEO) | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| CEO — Geoff E. Tanner (FY2025 PEO, departed Jan 18, 2026) | $825,000 (year-end FY2025 annualized) | Target $880,000 (110% of base); actual paid $748,000 (85% of target) | PSUs (50% of LTI $ value) + RSUs (50%); FY2025 grant-date fair value of stock awards $2,747,758 | Target mix: 59% long-term incentives (equity), 21% annual cash incentive, 20% base salary — i.e. ~59% equity / ~41% cash | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, Compensation Discussion & Analysis, p.40, 44-48; Summary Compensation Table, p.54] |
| CFO — Christopher J. Bealer (hired Mar 31, 2025; CFO eff. Jul 3, 2025) | $500,000 (annualized, from offer letter) | Target $171,900 (75%(1) of base, prorated); actual paid $158,100 (92% of target) | FY2025 (partial-year, hire-related) stock awards $840,000 (23,020 RSUs, off-cycle) + stock options $560,556 (34,035 options, per offer letter) | FY2025 total comp $2,026,776, of which $1,400,556 (69%) was equity (stock + option awards), reflecting a one-time hire grant, not the steady-state target mix | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, Summary Compensation Table p.54, and Grants of Plan-Based Awards Table p.55-56] |
| **Current CEO — Joseph E. Scalzo** (effective Jan 19, 2026) | **$1,100,000** annual base (web-sourced) | Not disclosed in data pool — no target-bonus % or metric found for the new employment agreement | One-time inducement award: option to purchase 2,000,000 shares at fair market value on the grant date, 8-year term, vesting ~1/3 per year over 3 years on the anniversary of his start date, subject to continued employment (no performance/vesting conditions tied to a financial or TSR target beyond the strike price itself) | Not fully quantifiable from pool data — the $2,000,000-share option alone, at even a modest per-share value, dwarfs the prior CEO's annual LTI grant | Base salary and inducement-grant terms: Web-sourced, unverified — SEC Form 8-K (Exhibit 10.1 Employment Agreement; Exhibit 10.2 Inducement Option), filed 2026-01-21, and secondary reporting (Globe and Mail press release, 2026-01-26), dated and labelled unverified; not directly in the data pool (only referenced by exhibit index in the Jul-09-2026 10-Q) |

(1) Bealer's FY2025 target incentive of $171,900 implies a 75% target-bonus rate against his $229,200 prorated base for the partial year worked (Mar 31–Aug 30, 2025); full-year annualized base is $500,000.

**Data-pool gap on the current CEO.** The 2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement's Compensation Discussion & Analysis covers fiscal year 2025 pay, when Tanner was CEO. Scalzo's actual employment agreement and inducement-option grant notice (SEC Form 8-K, filed 2026-01-21) are referenced by exhibit number in the Form 10-Q exhibit indexes in the pool but the exhibits themselves are not present in the data pool; the base-salary figure and equity-grant terms above are taken from a web search of the underlying SEC filing and are labelled unverified per this module's rules. No target annual-bonus percentage, no LTIP metric, and no say-on-pay data exist yet for Scalzo's package. This caps confidence on any forward-looking (post-January-2026) incentive read.

## 2. Performance Metrics

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| FY2025 Annual bonus (all NEOs, including Tanner and Bealer) | Net Sales (constant-currency) | 50% | **Size-based** — an absolute revenue-dollar target ($1,451.5M target for FY2025), not a per-share or margin metric | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Annual Performance-Based Cash Incentive Compensation," p.44-45] |
| FY2025 Annual bonus | Adjusted EBITDA (non-GAAP, constant-currency) | 50% | **Size-based** — an absolute EBITDA-dollar target ($281.3M target), not EBITDA-per-share, ROIC, or margin | same |
| FY2025 Annual bonus | Individual performance adjustment (discretionary, Compensation Committee/CEO recommendation) | ±25% of the financial-metric outcome | Neither — discretionary, non-formulaic overlay | same, p.44 |
| FY2025 Long-term incentive (LTI) — PSUs | Relative Total Shareholder Return (TSR) vs. Russell 3000 Food & Beverage index, 3-year performance period ending Oct 15, 2027 | Primary driver, 0%–200% payout scale (25th pctile=25%, 50th pctile=100%, 75th pctile=200%) | **Per-share/returns-based** — TSR is a genuine per-share, market-tested metric | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Long-Term Equity Incentive Compensation," p.46-47] |
| FY2025 LTI — PSUs | Consolidated Adjusted EBITDA compound annual growth rate (CAGR), same 3-year period, modifies the TSR outcome | Modifier: CEO ±12.5%/±25%; other NEOs ±25%/±50%, based on CAGR bands (<3% to ≥8%) | **Size-based (growth-rate)** — CAGR of an absolute EBITDA number, not EBITDA-per-share or ROIC; it overlays and can move the TSR-driven payout by up to 25 (CEO) or 50 (other NEOs) percentage points | same, p.47 |
| FY2025 LTI — RSUs (50% of LTI dollar value) | None — time-based vesting only (3 equal annual installments) | 50% of LTI grant value | Neither a returns metric nor a size metric — pure time-vesting; value is stock-price-linked by construction but requires no outperformance | same, p.47 |
| Current CEO (Scalzo) inducement option (Jan 2026) | None disclosed — time-based vesting only (1/3/year over 3 years); payoff requires the stock price to exceed the grant-date strike | 100% of the disclosed inducement grant | Loosely per-share (options only pay if the share price rises) but **no explicit performance hurdle, relative benchmark, or return target** — a blunt instrument compared with the relative-TSR PSU design used for the rest of the executive team | Web-sourced, unverified — SEC Form 8-K Exhibit 10.2, filed 2026-01-21 (not in data pool) |

**Read:** The annual cash bonus — 21%-23% of target NEO pay and paid every year — is **100% size-based** (net sales + absolute Adjusted EBITDA, no ROIC, ROCE, EPS, FCF-per-share, or margin metric anywhere in it). The long-term incentive is mixed: the PSU half (25% of total target CEO pay, i.e. 50% of the 59% LTI weight) is genuinely per-share via relative TSR, but that outcome can be moved up to 25-50 percentage points by an absolute Adjusted-EBITDA CAGR modifier that is not a per-share metric. The RSU half of the LTI (another ~25% of target CEO pay) carries no performance condition at all. The new CEO's headline 2026 equity award — 2,000,000 stock options, dwarfing any prior annual grant — has no performance metric beyond the option's own strike price, a materially weaker design than the relative-TSR PSU structure used for the rest of the team in FY2025.

## 3. Pay vs Performance

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| CEO total pay (latest, Summary Compensation Table basis) | Tanner (FY2025 PEO): $4,330,633 total (Salary $816,667 + Stock Awards $2,747,758 + Non-Equity Incentive $748,000 + All Other $18,208); up 5.9% from FY2024's $4,089,493 | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, Summary Compensation Table, p.54] |
| CEO "Compensation Actually Paid" (CAP, SEC pay-versus-performance basis) | FY2025 CAP to PEO $3,713,026 (vs SCT total $4,330,633) — the CAP measure marks unvested equity to market and rose 64.7% year-over-year (FY2024 CAP was $2,254,161) even though FY2025 net income fell ~25.6% (to $103.6M from $139.3M) — CAP tracked the Company's TSR (cumulative value of $100 invested: $112.76 at FY2025-end vs $106.01 at FY2024-end), not net income | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Pay versus Performance" table, p.62-63] |
| CEO pay ratio | 30:1 — Tanner's FY2025 total compensation ($4,330,633) vs. the median employee's ($146,036) | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "CEO Pay Ratio Disclosure," p.61] |
| Pay vs peers | Compensation Committee, advised by Mercer, targets executive pay "at or around the 50th percentile" of a 15-company peer group (B&G Foods, BellRing Brands, Celsius Holdings, Central Garden & Pet, Edgewell, e.l.f. Beauty, Freshpet, Inter Parfums, J&J Snack Foods, John B. Sanfilippo & Son, Lancaster Colony, The Boston Beer Company, The Hain Celestial Group, Tootsie Roll Industries, Utz Brands) selected on revenue size, enterprise value and net-income margin; no specific peer CEO dollar figures are disclosed in the pool to benchmark against | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Peer Companies," p.42-43] |
| Pay-for-performance alignment (FY2025 bonus outcome) | Company hit 99% of Net Sales target and 99% of Adjusted EBITDA target; the combined payment-methodology outcome was 94% of the financial-metric target (below the 100% payout line), and the Compensation Committee then applied a further 2% (all NEOs) and an incremental 9% (Tanner only) **discretionary reduction** "to provide additional bonus funding" for employees below the executive level — Tanner's final payout was 85% of his target, other NEOs 92% | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Annual Performance-Based Cash Incentive Compensation," p.44-45] |
| Pay-for-performance alignment (prior PSU vesting) | The November 2022 TSR-only PSU grant vested at 126.0% of target in November 2025, reflecting a 56.5th-percentile relative-TSR outcome versus the Russell 3000 Food & Beverage index — a formulaic, metric-driven outcome, not a discretionary one | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Fiscal Year 2025 PSU Vesting," p.48] |
| Say-on-pay support (%) | 97.3% approval at the January 2025 Annual Meeting for fiscal-year-2024 compensation (the most recent result the pool discloses); the vote on FY2025 compensation was scheduled for the January 28, 2026 Annual Meeting and its outcome is not yet in the data pool | [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, "Fiscal Year 2024 Say-on-Pay Voting Results," p.41] |
| CEO transition cost | Tanner's departure (Jan 18, 2026) and Scalzo's inducement package (2,000,000 stock options plus a $1,100,000 base, web-sourced) represent a material one-time compensation event outside the ordinary annual cycle; the data pool does not contain Tanner's severance terms or the full Scalzo package | [The-Simply-Good-Foods-Company-NasdaqCM-SMPL-Key-Developments.txt, entries dated Jan-20-2026 / Jan-21-2026]; inducement-grant size, Web-sourced (unverified), 2026-08-06 |

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

The annual cash bonus — the pay component executives are graded on every single year — pays 100% on net sales and absolute Adjusted EBITDA, both size metrics with no ROIC, EPS, FCF-per-share, or margin component; the Compensation Committee also used its discretion to cut the FY2025 bonus payout below the formula result to fund pay elsewhere in the organization, which is a governance positive (it shows discretion cutting, not padding, pay) but does not change what the formula itself rewards. The three-year PSU program is a real per-share mechanism — its primary driver is relative total shareholder return (TSR) against a food-and-beverage index — but that TSR outcome can be pushed up or down by as much as 25-50 percentage points by an absolute Adjusted-EBITDA growth-rate modifier, and the other half of the long-term award (RSUs) carries no performance condition at all. The single biggest incentive signal right now is the new CEO's 2,000,000-share, purely time-vesting inducement stock option (web-sourced, unverified pending the primary 8-K exhibit) — a package that abandons the relative-TSR PSU discipline used for the rest of the team in favor of a blunt "any share-price rise pays off" grant with no explicit performance hurdle, at a scale that dwarfs the prior CEO's entire annual long-term-incentive grant.

**Conclusion: mixed.** The bonus is size-based; the PSU sleeve of the LTI is genuinely per-share (TSR) with a size-based (EBITDA CAGR) overlay; the RSU sleeve and the new CEO's inducement option carry no performance conditions at all.

## Incentive Alignment Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Returns / per-share metrics | 12 | 25 | Annual bonus (21-23% of target pay) is 100% size-based (net sales + absolute Adjusted EBITDA); PSU sleeve of LTI (roughly 25-30% of target CEO pay) is genuinely per-share via relative TSR but diluted by an absolute EBITDA-CAGR modifier; RSU sleeve (another ~25-30% of target CEO pay) has no metric at all [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, p.44-47] |
| Metric clarity and weights | 16 | 20 | Metrics, weights, and payout tables (bonus 50/50 net sales/EBITDA; PSU TSR percentile table; EBITDA CAGR modifier table) are all explicitly disclosed with numeric thresholds — well above typical disclosure quality [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, p.44-47] |
| Pay-for-performance alignment | 14 | 20 | FY2025 bonus paid below target (85%-92%) on a 94% financial-metric outcome plus a discretionary cut; November 2022 PSU vested at a formulaic 126.0% tied to a real 56.5th-percentile TSR outcome; CAP data show pay tracking TSR, not net income, which fell ~25.6% in FY2025 [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, p.44-48, 62-63] |
| Long-term orientation | 12 | 15 | 59% of CEO target pay is long-term equity, vesting/performance periods run 3 years; new CEO's inducement option also vests over 3 years (8-year term), though it carries no performance metric [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, p.40, 46-47; web-sourced 8-K details] |
| Pay magnitude vs peers/performance | 6 | 10 | Policy targets the 50th percentile of a 15-company peer group (no verified peer $ figures in pool); new CEO's package (base $1.1M plus a 2,000,000-share option, web-sourced) is materially front-loaded relative to the outgoing CEO's steady-state annual grant, a magnitude signal this module cannot fully verify from primary filings |
| Minority-holder alignment | 8 | 10 | Anti-hedging/anti-pledging policy, dual clawback policies, 5x-base CEO stock-ownership guideline, annual say-on-pay vote with 97.3% support (FY2024 comp), no tax gross-ups, no excise-tax gross-ups on change-in-control [2026 Annual Meeting Proxy Statement, p.41, 50-51] |
| **Total** | **68** | **100** | Watchlist band (55-69) per this module's scoring bands — a mixed incentive design: strong disclosure and a credible relative-TSR PSU mechanism, offset by a 100%-size-based annual bonus, an unconditioned RSU sleeve, and material data gaps on the new CEO's package |

## management-governance / 04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md
